# R1/R2

[← Family leaderboard](../technique-inventory.md)

**Tags:** [adversarial-training](../technique-inventory.md#tag-adversarial-training) · [critic-gradient-penalty](../technique-inventory.md#tag-critic-gradient-penalty)

## Technique overview

The R1/R2 family now uses the Modern GAN training adaptation: paired relativistic logistic loss, native Adam, and squared critic input-gradient penalties on both real and fake samples. Its early schedule raises Adam's second-moment decay while reducing penalty strength. The retained older R1/R2 entry only swapped K3P's penalty and kept K3P's optimizer controls; it is a different complete recipe.

## Mathematical formulation

$G$ is the generator, $D$ the raw-score critic, $P$ the task prior, $x$ a real batch, $z\sim P$ a latent batch and $y$ the generated batch, including any declared output noise. $C$ is the critic actually evaluated: $C=D$ without input noise; otherwise every forward evaluates $D(u+\epsilon_{\mathrm{in}})$ with a fresh draw. $\mathbb E$ means a sample mean, $\lVert\cdot\rVert_2$ is the Euclidean norm, $(a)_+=\max(a,0)$ and $\operatorname{softplus}(a)=\log(1+e^a)$. The scalar sketch detaches $y$ during the critic update and resamples differentiable $y$ after that update; joint and auxiliary hosts retain task-owned objectives. $g_r=\nabla_x C(x)$ and $g_f=\nabla_y C(y)$ are per-sample input gradients; $d$ counts critic-input coordinates. $\lambda$ is penalty strength and $\kappa$ the cap threshold. $\gamma(t)$ is scheduled R1/R2 strength, $\beta_2(t)$ Adam second-moment decay, $t$ completed updates and $T$ the resolved recipe horizon. $c(t)$ is the early cosine interpolation weight.

**Default paired adversarial loss**

$$
\begin{aligned}\ell_D&=\mathbb E\!\left[\operatorname{softplus}(C(y)-C(x))\right],\\\ell_G&=\mathbb E\!\left[\operatorname{softplus}(C(x)-C(y))\right].\end{aligned}
$$

These scalar losses are minimized. The critic objective is $\ell_D+R$; the generator adds only its declared auxiliary objectives. Critic fakes are detached, and generator fakes and both scores are recomputed after the critic step. $C$ includes any declared fresh input noise.

**Squared real and fake input-gradient penalty**

$$
R_{\mathrm{R1/R2}}=\frac{\gamma(t)}{2}\left[\mathbb E\lVert g_r\rVert_2^2+\mathbb E\lVert g_f\rVert_2^2\right].
$$

R1 regularizes real-input gradients and R2 fake-input gradients. These are squared L2 norms, without a cap threshold, RMS normalization or EMA proximity. The modern canonical and selected recipes use native Adam; the retained older penalty-swap recipe has its own inherited K3P controls.

**Early moment and penalty schedules**

$$
\begin{aligned}c(t)&=\frac{1+\cos\!\left(\pi\min\!\left(1,\frac{t}{0.2T}\right)\right)}{2},\\\beta_2(t)&=0.99+(0.9-0.99)c(t),\\\gamma(t)&=0.1+(1-0.1)c(t).\end{aligned}
$$

Over the first $20\%$ of the resolved recipe horizon $T$, the second-moment decay moves from $0.9$ to $0.99$ while penalty strength moves from $1$ to $0.1$. Both then hold. This schedule is separate from LR: the canonical card keeps $0.0002$ constant, whereas the selected $0.0085$ variant uses its declared LR cosine.

## Simplified pseudocode

```text
For each training iteration:
  Apply the resolved LR schedule; cosine-interpolate beta2 and gamma over the first 20% of the task horizon.
  Draw real x and prior z; fake = detach(G(z)).
  Compute the paired critic adversarial loss shown above.
  g_r = gradient(D(x),x); g_f = gradient(D(fake),fake), using detached input copies.
  Add the fixed real/fake input-gradient penalty shown above.
  Backpropagate L_D; take a native Adam critic step with no spike guard or global clipping.
  Freeze D parameters; draw fresh z, regenerate fake and recompute both scores.
  Compute the paired generator adversarial loss from the updated critic; add only the host's declared auxiliary objective.
  Backpropagate L_G; update G and any learned prior with native Adam.
  Do not apply A2, critic anchoring, direct-particle gain or additive training noise.
  Score the live model with the frozen task's sampling law.
```

## Training details

| Characteristic | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Adversarial loss | Paired relativistic logistic, the RpGAN component of the Modern GAN baseline. Forge uses the host's toy architecture and prior; this is not a reproduction of the paper's image architecture or full Stacked MNIST experiment. |
| Optimizer | Modern canonical and selected cards use native torch.optim.Adam, $\beta_1=0$, $\varepsilon=10^{-8}$, AMSGrad off. $\beta_2$ cosine-interpolates $0.9$ to $0.99$ over the first $20\%$ of each resolved task horizon, then holds. Observer bookkeeping does not alter Adam's update. |
| Learning rates and annealing | Canonical modern card keeps $0.0002$ constant for $G$ and $D$ and learned prior. The selected whole variant uses $0.0085$, critic/prior multipliers $1$, no short network horizon cap, and cosine LR decay from $20\%$ of the resolved horizon to a $10\%$ floor. Exact host binding determines the horizon. |
| Parameter-gradient clipping | Disabled in modern canonical/selected cards: $\texttt{d\_guard\_ratio}=0$ and no global gradient-norm clip. The squared input-gradient penalty changes the critic loss rather than clipping parameter gradients. The historical K3P swap retains its inherited spike guard. |
| Critic penalties and anchors | Apply $\gamma(t)/2$ times the sum of real and fake mean squared L2 input-gradient norms. $\gamma$ cosine-interpolates from $1$ to $0.1$ over the first $20\%$ of the resolved horizon; apply the penalty each step. No cap threshold, RMS rescaling, LR handover or EMA proximity term is active. |
| Damping and update guards | Modern cards disable A2, direct-particle gain, critic spike guard and anchor. The older k3p-r1r2-matched-v1 keeps those K3P controls where its host supports them; they must not be attributed to the modern native-Adam baseline. |
| Training and sampling noise | Modern cards have zero additive critic-input and generated-output noise. A learned MoG may still have task-declared latent sampling noise; disabling training-output noise does not remove that prior law. |
| Parameter averaging and serving | Modern cards set generator/prior EMA decay to zero and use live scoring. The paper's EMA image evaluation is not imported. Historical K3P penalty-swap receipts retain their own averaging configuration. |

## Configuration differences

- Each result retains its executed source, recipe, task prior, initialization, budget and sampling law. These descriptions do not change or requalify recorded measurements.
- Task-owned objectives and active components matter: direct sample particles, learned latent rows and a generator network are different parameter roles. A declared recipe switch does not imply that every host can apply it.
- The selected r1r2--302b6baa44f629bfc97270c00a91f3cd6747585897bf43e105ab8aba2a276d6f was recorded at source 928b485ffbe6e17d79b41d307ae8b6275489b37a. Its .0085 rate and LR cosine differ from the canonical constant-.0002 card.
- Toy component hosts retain their original direct-particle, conditional, joint or reconstruction objectives. A shorter host horizon scales the declared schedules through the explicit binding; an external execution cap does not silently redefine them.
- The modern training adaptation, historical fixed R1/R2 swap and their recorded sources cannot pool successful cells or establish a single-change comparison.

## References

- [The GAN is dead; long live the GAN! A Modern GAN Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05441). RpGAN with R1/R2 and the modern training baseline; Forge adapts its training to toy hosts.
- [Which Training Methods for GANs do actually Converge?](https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.04406). Zero-centered input-gradient regularization; not the full repository formulation.
- [Adam: A Method for Stochastic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.6980). Base optimizer. Guard, damping and controller rules are repository additions.
